# Multi-session cache/coalescing simulation (offline, no model / no SSD)

All numbers below are simulator outputs (estimates/projects), derived from measured route data.
Row definitions:
- `uniq/step` = distinct (layer,expert) reads per coalesced layer step.
- `uniq/agg-tok` = distinct reads / (N x token positions); the sanity-anchor metric.
- `coalesced%` = duplicate same-layer requests removed by coalescing / raw requests; never a cache hit.
- `hit%` = cache hits / distinct reads; `MB/agg-tok` = misses x record_bytes / (N x tokens) / 1e6.
- `io_ms = MB/agg-tok / ssd_gbps` (+ Nemotron copy ms); perfect = 1000/max(gpu_ms,io_ms),
  none = 1000/(gpu_ms+io_ms). Limiter is the largest of SSD/GPU/copy ms.

## qwen

Traces: 3 sessions, token counts [256, 256, 256]. record_bytes=3211264 (measured).
Session state 216.1 MB/session (estimate); SSD 2.9 GB/s and GPU 100.0 ms/token (estimates).

Measured 8 pinned experts/layer are excluded; docs say 10 routed experts/layer (cited). `--topk-scale 1.25` reports the 10-expert case, labeled estimate.

## Notes and provenance

- All throughput figures are estimates/projections, not measurements; no checkpoint or SSD was used.
- measured: Qwen record_bytes=3211264, ~8 streamed experts/layer (8 pinned experts excluded from trace).
- cited: docs describe 10 routed experts/layer; --topk-scale 1.25 reports that case as an estimate.
- estimate: Nemotron record_bytes=5.61*1024*1024 per expert; session state 900/400 MB; SSD 2.9/3.3 GB/s; GPU 100/38 ms; copy 0.21 ms/expert (from cited 1.28 ms / 6 cached experts).
- idea: coalesced duplicates are not cache hits; lockstep coalesces same-layer requests, interleaved uses random layer phases with an in-flight window of --inflight-layers.
- The scheduler and arrangements are modelling constructs; they are not observed runtime schedules.
- Caveat: cells where N x session_state_mb exhausts cache_gb clamp to a 1-slot pool; the reused Belady policy's eviction rule can then underperform ARC (observed on Qwen C7/interleaved/N=4/cap 1-2 GB), so Belady is an upper bound only for non-degenerate capacities. The tiny-fixture unit test checks the normal case.

## Sanity anchors

- estimate (anchors): Nemotron B/lru/lockstep 6.6 GB: uniq/agg-tok N=1 138 vs N=4 113 (anchor 138 vs ~112); MB/agg-tok N=1 221 vs N=4 98 (anchor ~190-200 at 6.6 GB, ~280-290 at 4.4 GB). Difference: this model subtracts N x session_state_mb from cache GB and coalesces all same-layer requests in lockstep; the quick-replay anchor did not state either assumption.
- estimate (anchors): Nemotron B/lru/lockstep 4.4 GB: uniq/agg-tok N=1 138 vs N=4 113 (anchor 138 vs ~112); MB/agg-tok N=1 302 vs N=4 226 (anchor ~190-200 at 6.6 GB, ~280-290 at 4.4 GB). Difference: this model subtracts N x session_state_mb from cache GB and coalesces all same-layer requests in lockstep; the quick-replay anchor did not state either assumption.
- estimate (anchor): Qwen C(k=7)/lru with the fixed 349-slot pool (cited production size): MB/agg-tok N=1 1.22 GB vs N=3 0.95 GB (anchor ~1.17 vs ~0.9 GB).
- cited: cross-conversation same-layer share 0.0627 from results/flashnext/20261114-132303-route-overlap-manual/route-overlap.json.
- idea: A is an upper-ish bound (prompts are similar); D is a lower bound; B is the main unrelated-sub-agent case; C is the shared-context proxy.
